class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
                undefinedFunction(word)
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        if not forbidden:
            return len(word)

        def forbidden_prefix_length(i: int, j: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            limit = min(j, i + max_len)
            for pos in range(i, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - i + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_length(i, j)
            if length:
                j = i + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)
        return ans
```

Let's recall LC 2781 official solution: Use set of forbidden strings, and for each right index, check all suffixes ending at right up to maxLen. Maintain left. That is O(n*maxLen^2?) Actually slicing creates substrings of length up to maxLen, total O(n*maxLen^2?) if slicing length L costs L, but maxLen <=10 so fine. Simpler:

```python
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        forbidden_set = set(forbidden)
        max_len = max(len(f) for f in forbidden)
        left = 0
        ans = 0
        for right in range(len(word)):
            for k in range(1, min(max_len, right-left+1)+1):
                if word[right-k+1:right+1] in forbidden_set:
                    left = right-k+2
                    break
            ans = max(ans, right-left+1)
        return ans
```

This is standard. But the original code uses trie and backward. Which one should we output? The prompt specifically says "Observe the following faulty python code. It contains one or more bugs. The problem: Given a string word and a list of forbidden strings, return length of longest valid substring. Constraints forbidden[i].length <= 10. Many solutions use a set and sliding window. Another approach: Sort forbidden by length? The original code might be from a user with a bug. The intended fix maybe:

```python
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            node = trie
            for ch in f:
                if ch not in node:
                    node[ch] = {}
                node = node[ch]
            node["end"] = True

        if max_len == 0:
            return len(word)

        n = len(word)

        def forbidden_prefix_length(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            node = trie
            limit = min(end, start + max_len)
            for pos in range(start, limit):
                ch = word[pos]
                if ch not in node:
                    return 0
                node = node[ch]
                if "end" in node:
                    return pos - start + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        right = n
        for left in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_length(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, right - left)
        return ans
```

This is clean. Note variable `right` is exclusive. Good.

Not relevant. Could handle by if any f == "": return 0. But constraints no. We can ignore. To be robust, maybe:

Let's evaluate if it handles all cases. Standard algorithm:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        forbidden_set = set(forbidden)
        max_len = max(len(f) for f in forbidden) if forbidden else 0
        left = 0
        ans = 0
        for right in range(len(word)):
            for k in range(1, min(max_len, right - left + 1) + 1):
                if word[right - k + 1:right + 1] in forbidden_set:
                    left = right - k + 2
                    break
            ans = max(ans, right - left + 1)
        return ans
```

Let's test with examples. word="abc", forbidden=["ab","bc"]:
right0 'a': k=1 "a" not, ans1
right1 'b': k=1 "b" not, k=2 "ab" yes left=1? right-k+2 =1-2+2=1, break, ans=max(1,1)=1
right2 'c': k=1 "c" not, k=2 "bc" yes left=2-2+2=2, break, ans=max(1,1)=1. Correct.
word="abc", forbidden=["abc","bc"]:
right0 'a' ans1
right1 'b': k1 "b" no, k2 "ab" no, ans2
right2 'c': k1 "c" no, k2 "bc" yes left=2, ans=max(2,1)=2. Correct.
word="abcde", forbidden=["abcd","cde"]:
right0 a ans1
right1 ab ans2
right2 abc ans3
right3 abcd: k1 d no,k2 cd no,k3 bcd no,k4 abcd yes left=0? right-k+2=3-4+2=1, break, ans=max(3,3)=3
right4 abcde: k1 e no,k2 de no,k3 cde yes left=4-3+2=3, break, ans=max(3,2)=3. Correct.
word="aaaa", forbidden=["aa"]:
right0 a ans1
right1 a: k1 a no,k2 aa yes left=0? right-k+2=1-2+2=1, break, ans=max(1,1)=1
right2 a: k1 a no,k2 aa yes left=1, ans1
right3 a: k1 a no,k2 aa yes left=2, ans1. Correct.

This set-based algorithm is O(n*max_len) and memory O(forbidden). It is the standard solution. It doesn't use trie. Would it be considered "fixed code" for the given faulty code? Now, let's think about if the trie/backward code could be further simplified by using a set and checking suffixes in the backward loop. For each left, check prefixes of word[left:right] up to max_len against forbidden set. That would be similar:

```python
forbidden_set = set(forbidden)
max_len = max(len(f) for f in forbidden)
right = len(word)
ans = 0
for left in range(len(word)-1, -1, -1):
    for k in range(1, min(max_len, right-left)+1):
        if word[left:left+k] in forbidden_set:
            right = left + k - 1
            break
    ans = max(ans, right-left)
return ans
```

Then max_len = max(len(f) for f in forbidden) (>=1). This is cleaner. Let's structure:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        if not forbidden:
            return len(word)

        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            node = trie
            for ch in f:
                if ch not in node:
                    node[ch] = {}
                node = node[ch]
            node["end"] = True

        n = len(word)

        def forbidden_prefix_length(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            node = trie
            limit = min(end, start + max_len)
            for pos in range(start, limit):
                ch = word[pos]
                if ch not in node:
                    return 0
                node = node[ch]
                if "end" in node:
                    return pos - start + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        right = n
        for left in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_length(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, right - left)
        return ans
```

This handles empty forbidden. If forbidden contains empty string, max_len=0, but `if not forbidden` false, max_len=0, `forbidden_prefix_length` limit=min(end,start), returns 0, so ans would be len(word), wrong. But constraints no empty. If we want robust against empty string, we can add:

```python
if any(f == "" for f in forbidden):
    return 0
```

But not necessary and might be considered extra. The original code would also mishandle empty forbidden? If forbidden=[], trie empty, isForbidden false, res=n. If forbidden=[""], trie root["end"]=True, isForbidden(word[i:j]) would check first char? Actually isForbidden(s) starts t=trie, for c in s: if c not in t: ... It never checks root "end" before iterating. If s non-empty, c not in t? root has "end" key but not character c, so returns False. So original code would return n for forbidden=[""], which is wrong. But constraints no empty. Fine.

Now, let's think about if the code's `max_len` should be `max(len(f) for f in forbidden)` but if forbidden list contains strings longer than word, no issue. The loop limit min(end, start+max_len) <= n. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `forbidden_prefix_length` returns 0 if no forbidden prefix. But if a forbidden word is found at length `length`, and there is a shorter forbidden word that is also a prefix but not "end" because the trie insertion didn't set "end" due to duplicate? It sets. Good.

Now, let's consider if the set/backward code might be easier for the final answer because it's shorter and less memory. But I think trie/backward is fine.

But constraints max_len <=10. If not, original code O(n^2) anyway. The set-based sliding window also O(n*max_len). So okay.

Let's maybe write the final code with no comments, plain. Ensure indentation.

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        if not forbidden:
            return len(word)

        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            node = trie
            for ch in f:
                if ch not in node:
                    node[ch] = {}
                node = node[ch]
            node["end"] = True

        n = len(word)

        def forbidden_prefix_length(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            node = trie
            limit = min(end, start + max_len)
            for pos in range(start, limit):
                ch = word[pos]
                if ch not in node:
                    return 0
                node = node[ch]
                if "end" in node:
                    return pos - start + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        right = n
        for left in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_length(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, right - left)

        return ans
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        if not forbidden:
            return len(word)

        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            node = trie
            for ch in f:
                if ch not in node:
                    node[ch] = {}
                node = node[ch]
            node["end"] = True

        n = len(word)

        def forbidden_prefix_length(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            node = trie
            limit = min(end, start + max_len)
            for pos in range(start, limit):
                ch = word[pos]
                if ch not in node:
                    return 0
                node = node[ch]
                if "end" in node:
                    return pos - start + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        right = n
        for left in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_length(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, right - left)

        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        if not forbidden:
            return len(word)

        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            node = trie
            for ch in f:
                if ch not in node:
                    node[ch] = {}
                node = node[ch]
            node["end"] = True

        n = len(word)

        def forbidden_prefix_length(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            node = trie
            limit = min(end, start + max_len)
            for pos in range(start, limit):
                ch = word[pos]
                if ch not in node:
                    return 0
                node = node[ch]
                if "end" in node:
                    return pos - start + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        right = n
        for left in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_length(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, right - left)

        return ans
